  <div class="abstract reveal d2">
    <div class="ab-k">Abstract</div>
    <div class="ab-b">Madwell LLC operated a financial extraction architecture from 2017 through 2024 that suppressed approximately <i>$141.5M of measured client receipts</i> from US tax returns and moved approximately <i>$136.2M of unattributable outflow</i> through eight named platform rails. The inflow gap, the outflow decomposition, and the $4.75M Bank of America Working Capital Loan resolve into one arithmetic identity — <i>the suppression equals the rails&rsquo; extraction plus the credit-line residual</i> that the four Obligor LLCs owed when Sojka became their sole member at the 10/3/2024 exit. The package establishes architecture at the method level for every rail and reproduces every dollar figure from primary source via <i>python3 verify.py</i>; act-level attribution at trial grade requires production from a small set of institutions itemized in §10.</div>
  </div>
  <div class="srcref reveal d3"><b>SOURCE</b> · START_HERE.md (thesis) · Summary.md §1–§2 · condensed without alteration of figures</div>
</section>

<!-- ============ PLATE 01 — THE EQUATION ============ -->
<section class="plate wrap" id="s01">
  <header class="popen reveal"><div class="po-num">01</div><div class="po-main"><h2 class="po-title">The Equation</h2><div class="po-coord">PLATE 01 · THE CLOSURE IDENTITY · FINDING A.6 · §2.8</div><p class="po-lede">three perimeters, independently measured — closing on each other at 0.42%</p></div></header>
  <p class="ph-kicker">Each perimeter was reconciled inside its own section before the cross-section identity was computed: §2.1 reconciles three witnesses on inflow; §2.2.3 reconciles two methods on outflow; §7 reproduces the bank&rsquo;s own Recital A on debt. A.6 is the cross-section reconciliation — the three headline aggregates close as a single equation.</p>

  <div class="stats reveal">
    <div class="stat"><div class="sv">$141,547,013</div><div class="sk">Inflow gap · 2018–2023 · §2.1</div></div>
    <div class="stat a"><div class="sv">$136,202,381</div><div class="sk">Eight-rail outflow · 2018–2024 · §2.2</div></div>
    <div class="stat"><div class="sv">$4,750,000</div><div class="sk">BoA loan outstanding 10/31/2023 · §7</div></div>

<!-- ============ PLATE 02 — SUPERPOSITION ============ -->
<section class="plate wrap" id="s02">
  <header class="popen reveal"><div class="po-num">02</div><div class="po-main"><h2 class="po-title">Superposition</h2><div class="po-coord">PLATE 02 · UNREPORTED REVENUE · §2.1 · SIX FILED YEARS</div><p class="po-lede">each system reconciles within itself — the disagreements live only at the boundaries</p></div></header>
  <p class="ph-kicker">The package&rsquo;s name for the condition: <b>fiscal superposition</b>. A single transaction is recorded as one thing in QuickBooks, another in the bank statement, a third in the reconciliation file, a fourth in the tax filing — each record internally consistent. For seven years, no one compared the systems side by side. The discipline throughout: what a bank or custodian <b>stamped</b> (amount, date, reference, account number) is fact; what the operator <b>typed</b> (payee name, memo, label) is the operator&rsquo;s narrative, validated by no one.</p>

  <div class="frame reveal" style="margin-top:44px;max-width:880px">
    <span class="tk tl"></span><span class="tk tr"></span><span class="tk bl"></span><span class="tk br"></span>

  <div class="cards3 reveal d1">
    <div class="card"><div class="cl">Witness 1</div><h4>Delgado — bank-side</h4><p>Primary bank statements (BoA US, BoA HK, Chase subsidiaries) + IRS Form 1065 filings. Foxhunt Financial forensic reconstruction, EX-58.</p><div class="kv"><div class="k">Client-derived inflows</div><div class="v">$279,551,475</div></div></div>
    <div class="card"><div class="cl">Witness 2</div><h4>Tucker — year-end internals</h4><p>Sum of Tucker&rsquo;s own six internal income statements 2018–2023, extracted from the &ldquo;Financials for David&rdquo; hierarchy and the January-closing folders.</p><div class="kv"><div class="k">Total Income</div><div class="v">$279,544,475</div></div></div>
    <div class="card"><div class="cl">Witness 3</div><h4>Tucker — Gross Profit accumulator</h4><p>Parallel &ldquo;Gross Profit&rdquo; series maintained in Tucker&rsquo;s workpapers — an independent internal surplus measurement.</p><div class="kv"><div class="k">Internal surplus</div><div class="v">$135,943,334</div></div></div>
  </div>

  <div class="frame note reveal d2" style="margin-top:26px">
    <div class="frame-head"><span><span class="otag">NOTE</span> <b>expense-side absorption — same architecture from two ends</b></span><span class="fig">§2.1 / §4.3.10 / §4.6.10</span></div>
    <p style="font-size:13.5px;color:var(--ink-2);line-height:1.65">A suppressed inflow does not balance on its own. The expense-side counterpart is the <b>Payroll/COGS concealment rail</b> — gross labor and media inflated above actual cash cost, parked in COGS, offset by a named dual-book contra. US labor limb: wage P&amp;L at ~2× actual JustWorks W-3, with <b>59999 · COGS Elimination</b> as the pure-credit contra at $4,681,099 in 2019. HK media limb: Hylink markup ~HK$18M; a <b>manufactured loss of −HK$9,152,055</b> on the 2021 HK income statement holds ~US$19M of Danone margin off the US return. Cash that came in and was never booked is balanced by expense booked at a multiple of actual cost.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="takeaway reveal">
    <div class="tw-k">Takeaway · Plate 02</div>
    <div class="tw-t"><b>$141.5M of measured inflow does not appear on any US tax return.</b> Every year shows the same pattern.</div>
    <div class="tw-s">Stated verbatim at §2.1 — six confirmed filed years</div>

Tucker sole reconciler.</div></div>

No Sojka personal guaranty added — the consolidation of equity ownership was the collection mechanism. <b>$4.75M residual = the closure-identity residual.</b></div></div>

    <div class="card"><div class="cl">Trigger I · 5/1/2023</div><h4>CashPro BillPay retired</h4><p>BoA retired the bundling product that had absorbed every Bill.com vendor payment into single-line &ldquo;Bill.com&rdquo; debits since 2018. Tucker, internally, same morning: <i>&ldquo;I don&rsquo;t think we use this, so we should be fine.&rdquo;</i> Account 1072 annual volume then contracted ~75%.</p><div class="kv"><div class="k">Section</div><div class="v">§4.1</div></div></div>
    <div class="card"><div class="cl">Trigger II · 3/14/2024</div><h4>Plan 99519 deconverts</h4><p>The entire Madwell 401(k) liquidated to Bright Future PEP at Transamerica via Extensis. The EXTENSIS rail — $13.99M / 101 entries, zero in every prior year — starts the moment the deconversion completes. Same architecture, new platform shell.</p><div class="kv"><div class="k">Section</div><div class="v">§4.3.8 · B.68</div></div></div>
    <div class="card"><div class="cl">The signature</div><h4>Chassis rotation</h4><p>2018–2023 composition: JustWorks + AMEX + Tricor = $114.7M / 86%. The 2024 composition has none of those — EXTENSIS + Divvy + AMEX tail ≈ $17.96M ≈ the 2024 aggregate. <b>The architecture does not stop in 2024. It rotates rails to the platforms still available.</b></p><div class="kv"><div class="k">Section</div><div class="v">§2.2.1</div></div></div>

<!-- ============ PLATE 05 — MECHANISM CATALOGUE ============ -->
<section class="plate wrap" id="s05">
  <header class="popen reveal"><div class="po-num">05</div><div class="po-main"><h2 class="po-title">Mechanism Catalogue</h2><div class="po-coord">PLATE 05 · MECHANISMS · §4.1–§4.10 · TWELVE INSTRUMENTS</div><p class="po-lede">one pattern, twelve instruments — each internally reconciled, each disagreeing only at its boundary</p></div></header>
  <p class="ph-kicker">The §11.5 universal pattern: every rail uses a <b>paired flow</b> — a visible outbound movement canceled by an invisible or differently-coded offset that reconciles in a separate ledger. No single audit perimeter consolidates both ledgers. Expand any component for the documentary detail.</p>

  <div class="kick reveal">The twelve instruments (12)</div>
  <div class="mech reveal">
    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Bridge</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">CashPro BillPay bundling</div><div class="cid">§4.1 · B.1 / B.7</div></div>
      <div class="cb">BoA commercial product, powered by Bill.com, that bundled individual vendor payments into single-line &ldquo;Bill.com&rdquo; bank debits — invisible at the statement layer by design. Bundle overshoot: payments exceeded same-day funds transfers on 401 of 624 days.</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">Overshoot</span><span class="v">$28,298,966</span></div><div><span class="k">Window</span><span class="v">2018–2023</span></div><div><span class="k">Retired</span><span class="v">5/1/2023</span></div><div><span class="k">1072 volume after</span><span class="v">−75%</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p>Sept 2018, same email chain, fifteen days apart: BoA confirms Madwell is already using Bill.com; Tucker writes to Sojka — </p>
        <div class="q">&ldquo;you don&rsquo;t need Bill.com. You are really using Intuit not Bill.com, but the bank statements don&rsquo;t show it clearly.&rdquo;<span class="who">Tucker → Sojka · Sept 2018 · §4.1</span></div>
        <div class="q">&ldquo;I don&rsquo;t think we use this, so we should be fine.&rdquo;<span class="who">Tucker, forwarding the retirement notice · 3/1/2023</span></div>
        <p>The two statements sit in the same record set and cannot both be true.</p>
      </details>
    </div>

    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Loop</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">Cannabis-property loop</div><div class="cid">§4.2 · B.34</div></div>
      <div class="cb">Madwell pays &ldquo;rent&rdquo; via Bill.com to property cos (GF 620 Park, 195 Throckmorton); their Chase accounts fund cannabis entities&rsquo; Divvy accounts (Goldfinch, CSDE — never broke ground); Madwell books the costs. Provides the $4M basis component of the $8M shelter.</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">Identified wires</span><span class="v">$7,500,000</span></div><div><span class="k">195 Throck 5/16/22</span><span class="v">$2,600,000</span></div><div><span class="k">GF 620 in SAG window</span><span class="v">$613,633 / 5</span></div><div><span class="k">CSDE draws (Sojka 0%)</span><span class="v">$3,808,947</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p>Cross River / Divvy DSAs signed by Tucker as CFO: Goldfinch 3/28/2023 20:56:27 UTC and CSDE 3/28/2023 21:08:59 + 4/13/2023, all from IP <b>208.255.74.70</b>, Chase-backed (parent Madwell DSA: 12/7/2022, IP 173.70.231.128, FinCEN BO cert lists Eisenman only — omits Sojka, a 50% holder). BoA Loan Agreement §8.20 contains a cannabis prohibition covenant. The B.34 payments (4/26–5/24/2024, $50,000 / $150,808 / $192,825 + two totaling $220,000) ran through Account 1072 inside the Special Assets Group window. Lease guaranty lists Sojka at 101 Decatur Street — an address he left in December 2019.</p>
      </details>
    </div>

    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Retirement layer</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">401(k) — Source 04 phantom-PE channel</div><div class="cid">§4.3 · B.25–B.27 · B.70</div></div>
      <div class="cb">Plan 99519 ran as two FPRS sub-instances. 99519-001 carried $3,192,850 of discretionary profit-sharing (Source 04) funded from Madwell&rsquo;s operating account and attributed to two single-member LLCs with no employees (GIGO d/b/a Bankrobber, MSJ d/b/a Starfish).</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">99519-001</span><span class="v">$3,192,850 / 99</span></div><div><span class="k">99519-002</span><span class="v">$526,776 / 20</span></div><div><span class="k">GIGO+MSJ payroll ACHes</span><span class="v">0 across 900 rows</span></div><div><span class="k">Receiving side (B.70)</span><span class="v">No Source 04</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p>Justworks stepped out of the data flow in writing (11/23/2020): <i>&ldquo;Justworks does not actually provide data transfers in instances like this&hellip;&rdquo;</i> From then on Tucker was the sole person telling Fidelity what to withdraw and how to attribute it. Plan setup decision: <i>&ldquo;No profit share to start. As our testing improves, Madwell will revisit.&rdquo;</i> Never operationally amended. Transamerica receiving-side record (Case 935008-00536): total employer-side money <b>$930,589</b>; the $3.19M Source 04 channel is <b>3.4× the entire employer balance and it is not there</b> — eliminates allocation-to-employees and suspense hypotheses; leaves Eisenman-§415-excess or exit-before-rollover. <b>Fidelity Record 77 is determinative</b> (subpoena priority #1). Forfeiture mechanic (§4.3.6): 6-year graded vesting for post-2021 hires; forfeiture account addressed &ldquo;C/O DANIEL TUCKER&rdquo;; §11.09(b) applies forfeitures to reduce next year&rsquo;s employer obligation — $1 forfeited in Year N = $1 Madwell doesn&rsquo;t pay in Year N+1, invisible at every gross-reporting perimeter.</p>
      </details>
    </div>

    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Books rail · US</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">Payroll/COGS rail — US labor limb</div><div class="cid">§4.3.10 · B.65</div></div>
      <div class="cb">Wage expense carried in two parallel sibling accounts — 60000 · Staffing + 61000 · Wages — with 61000 suppressed from the GL header index in the firm-of-record&rsquo;s own deliverables. Combined wage P&amp;L runs ~2× actual W-3 cash payroll every operative year.</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">2018 baseline</span><span class="v">1.01×</span></div><div><span class="k">2019→2023</span><span class="v">1.88 / 2.00 / 2.69 / 1.69 / 1.70×</span></div><div><span class="k">59999 contra (2019)</span><span class="v">−$4,681,099</span></div><div><span class="k">2022 W-3 anchor</span><span class="v">$19.02M vs $32.19M GL</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p><b>The activation moment:</b> General Journal entry, 12/31/2019, memo <i>&ldquo;Reclassify for Tax year 2019&rdquo;</i> — <b>$11,052,485.63</b> moved 60000 → 61000 at Tucker&rsquo;s first full close cycle as CFO. None of 59999 · COGS Elimination / 67050 · US Cost Allocation to HK / sibling-61000 exist in the 2018 books. 59999 is a pure-credit account: $0 debits, $4,681,099 credits — wipes COGS on the income statement while the gross inflation stays in the GL. M&amp;S Global&rsquo;s ms_gl CSVs: 61000 appears in the split column of 1,309 rows (2022) / 1,164 rows (2023) — 98.1% memo-tagged &ldquo;Justwork&hellip;&rdquo; — and as the account header of <b>zero</b> rows. The architecture has an author (Tucker), a year (2019), and a journal entry.</p>
      </details>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="mech reveal">
    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Tax credit</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">ERTC — the &ldquo;DT Playground&rdquo; base</div><div class="cid">§4.4.1 · B.66</div></div>
      <div class="cb">$2.51M of Employee Retention Credits at the 50% rate implies a $5.02M qualified-wages base. The base was built in a three-version workbook trail — the middle version carrying Tucker&rsquo;s own characterization in the filename: &ldquo;DT Playground.&rdquo;</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">Sep 24 2020</span><span class="v">95 emp · 218,210.65 h</span></div><div><span class="k">Sep 26 &ldquo;Playground&rdquo;</span><span class="v">249 emp · 388,547.77 h</span></div><div><span class="k">Oct 9 V2 (shipped)</span><span class="v">256 emp · 412,407.77 h</span></div><div><span class="k">Expansion</span><span class="v">+154 emp / +78% / 2 days</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p>For &gt;100-employee employers, 2020 ERTC counted only wages paid to employees <i>not providing services</i> — operationally, the nonbillable-hour share. The 154 employees added in two days are back-office/executive headcount that never logged time in Harvest. Department expansions: Media +96%, Production +93%, Design +89%, Creative +88%, Technology +88%, Strategy +84%, Copywriting +76%, Client Services +39%. The prep window (Sep–Oct 2020) predates the 12/27/2020 Consolidated Appropriations Act that made PPP recipients ERTC-eligible — the basis was built before it could be claimed.</p>
      </details>
    </div>

    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Certification</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">Anchin — five positions, one CFS</div><div class="cid">§4.4 · B.28 · B.47 · B.72</div></div>
      <div class="cb">July 12, 2022: Adweek runs the layoff with Eisenman on record (&ldquo;has grown consistently, retaining our team through ups and downs—and even the worst of the pandemic&rdquo;). July 14: Anchin issues the 2021 CFS — COVID &ldquo;not adversely impacted&rdquo; boilerplate, $4,090,884 PPP forgiveness + ~$2.5M ERC (both requiring adverse impact), no layoff disclosure inside the subsequent-events window. The five positions cannot all be true.</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">PPP1+PPP2 forgiven</span><span class="v">$4,090,884</span></div><div><span class="k">ERC as Other Income</span><span class="v">~$2,500,000</span></div><div><span class="k">Facility escalation</span><span class="v">$2.4M→$6M +$1.5M</span></div><div><span class="k">Engagement partner</span><span class="v">Belfer · 2017–2024</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p><b>The $8M shelter thread (1/13–15/2024, B.47):</b> Belfer confirms the $2,847,000 stock-loss carryover; instructs <i>&ldquo;you can REALIZE your losses on the stock account to offset the gain&rdquo;</i>; staff confirm the −$1,140,137 K-1 ending balance; Eisenman: <i>&ldquo;So essentially I don&rsquo;t pay taxes on my first 8m in gains, give or take a few hundred k.&rdquo;</i> Belfer: <i>&ldquo;<b>Pretty much.</b>&rdquo;</i> Components $4M cannabis basis + $2,847,000 carryover + $1,140,137 K-1 basis = $7,987,137 — 99.84% of the $8M target. <b>The engagement loop (B.72):</b> 2017 placement letter putting Tucker into Madwell — Eisenman-only, with the carve-out: <i>&ldquo;we will not be responsible for the misconduct or defalcations of any candidate you elect to hire&rdquo;</i> — through six years of iterative reviews to the 9/4/2024 signing of the 2023 1065. One engagement, one partner, one operator placed and certified year after year.</p>
      </details>
    </div>

    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">HR ledger</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">The layoff arc</div><div class="cid">§4.5 · B.29</div></div>
      <div class="cb">July 2022: coordinated RIF — Sojka authors internal messaging; Eisenman takes the press (his only on-record quote of the co-CEO era); the sibling-entity blurb naming Bankrobber/Starfish is cut at Eisenman&rsquo;s prompt; counsel&rsquo;s script is overridden in writing; the Controller who created the cannabis-loop Bill.com payments is offboarded one day after the CFS subsequent-events cutoff.</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">RIF</span><span class="v">~10% of ~160</span></div><div><span class="k">Gorkin separated</span><span class="v">7/15/2022</span></div><div><span class="k">2023 repeat</span><span class="v">blocked by Sojka</span></div><div><span class="k">Meanwhile hired</span><span class="v">+$1M payroll · 32 raises</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p>2023 attempt: Ball told her job was eliminated with &ldquo;incredible sense of urgency&rdquo;; no terminations follow; ~$1,000,000 of new-hire payroll and 32 raises (~$50K/month) are added instead. Eisenman 10/3/2023: <i>&ldquo;our financial situation was dire until we magically closed hylands, opentext, Elf Incremental, and branca incremental all in an 8-week period - it was not made up.&rdquo;</i></p>

        <p>Sherman, 10/4/2023: <i>&ldquo;you asked me to let Whitney go, alluding to Chris&rsquo;s agreement with the plan, and I did. Only to find out that Chris was not aligned - and Whitney was not let go.&rdquo;</i></p>

    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Offshore</div><div class="ct"><div class="cn">Hong Kong — three thefts + the rail</div><div class="cid">§4.6 · B.30 · B.55–B.58 · B.67</div></div>
      <div class="cb">Theft 1: Danone billings HK$193,464,678 (US$24.8M) — only $5.78M booked US-side, $1.79M returned, ~$19M never booked. Theft 2: Hylink markup ~HK$18M across 6 projects, 98.4% of flow offshore-routed. Theft 3: $6.27M of US wires never returned; Account 16030 washed to $0.00 across 438 JEs. Beneath it: a secret Shanghai WFOE (Dec 2019 – Sept 2023), Sojka on the registration in no capacity.</div>
      <div class="cspec"><div><span class="k">747 payments (B.56)</span><span class="v">HK$157,102,226.63</span></div><div><span class="k">Ledger reconcile</span><span class="v">746/747 · 99.9%</span></div><div><span class="k">Recipients confirmed</span><span class="v">0 of 747</span></div><div><span class="k">2021 HK net income</span><span class="v">−HK$9,152,055</span></div></div>
      <details><summary>Documentary detail</summary>
        <p>One commingled account (BoA HK 72440013) took Danone HK$193.46M + US-intercompany HK$61.78M and drained near zero. FPS routes on account number + branch code; the beneficiary <b>name</b> is sender-typed and bank-unvalidated (BoA&rsquo;s own FAQ, EX-86; alias-based confirmation &ldquo;out of scope&rdquo;). The CashPro alert corpus is operator-named (&ldquo;(Hong Kong Notification)&rdquo;), self-routed to dtucker@madwell.com on 150 of 150 messages — a reliable transaction register and a worthless payee register. 37.0% — HK$58,184,579 — routed to a single account suffix (…8006).</p>
        <div class="q">&ldquo;We have negotiated with Hylink for a lower commission instead of rebate. We want to avoid any records or even discussion of rebates in the Danone case.&rdquo;<span class="who">Alice Too, MD Madwell HK · 5/14/2019</span></div>
        <div class="q">&ldquo;Difference due to Intercompany accrual&hellip; Dan has suggested us to not record this intercompany transactions in the Books of Madwell LLC on monthly basis&hellip;&rdquo;<span class="who">M&amp;S Global workpaper comment field · April 2019</span></div>
        <p><b>HK media limb (B.67):</b> HK labor is clean at 1.02× (diagnostic — HK is not a labor shop); the inflation runs through media COGS instead. The −HK$9,152,055 manufactured loss on HK$28.5M of 2021 reported income is the books-side mechanism holding ~US$19M of Danone margin off the US return. <b>Tricor sibling (B.71):</b> twelve monthly GJ entries gross bank charges to HKD ~2,000 against ~HKD 116 actual (~206×), arithmetic in the memo field (&ldquo;(2000-11.5)&rdquo;), JE-template swap after an explicit VOID — a standing-instruction signature.</p>
      </details>

Predicate: 1711 + 2105 (+ later 6973) lived under a single Online ID — documented by dual statement notifications 9/2/2011 (same minute) and dual passcode-reset alerts 11/11/2018 (one minute apart).</p>

      <div class="cb">On the return Belfer signed 9/4/2024 — authorized by Eisenman as &ldquo;MANAGING MEMBER,&rdquo; a title that does not exist in the operating agreement, 29 days before the buyout — five engineered moves land at once.</div>

Lines that should be 50/50 are 50/50 within $1 — the asymmetry concentrates exclusively on the shelter-feeding lines. <b>B.80:</b> Form 8275 discloses the §199A position — Madwell, &ldquo;PROVIDING PUBLIC RELATIONS SERVICES,&rdquo; claims the de minimis exception on $557,925 / 2.4% consulting revenue. <b>B.83:</b> §6221(b) election-out routes any audit deficiency to partners-of-record — post-exit, practically, to Sojka alone. <b>B.84:</b> six engagement instruments 2017–2024, five invented titles, a silent President→CEO swap, and a partner rotation (Belfer→Rosenblatt) at the moment of collapse.</p>
      </details>

<section class="plate wrap" id="s06">
  <header class="popen reveal"><div class="po-num">06</div><div class="po-main"><h2 class="po-title">The AMEX Loop</h2><div class="po-coord">PLATE 06 · ANNEX A · THE AMEX LOOP · EIGHT FOUNDATIONS</div><p class="po-lede">the longest-running mechanism — 2012 through 2024 — a self-cleaning circuit on the face of the bank record</p></div></header>
  <p class="ph-kicker">A Madwell-corporate American Express card in the name of <b>Bernard D. Malaky — a non-employee</b> — as the conduit of a money-routing loop through Madwell&rsquo;s BoA footprint. The loop predates CFO Daniel Tucker by six years; he inherited it and professionalized its control surface. Eight convergent evidentiary foundations; the receiving card&rsquo;s own statements never enter the records the controlling shareholder can see.</p>

      <div class="cb"><b>Alice Too</b> (MD, Madwell HK) — signed the Hylink contracts and the 15-employee IR56B; the &ldquo;avoid any records&hellip;of rebates&rdquo; email; HK$190,000/month against a staff median near HK$30,000. <b>Serina Tsou</b> (GM, Hylink HK) — counterparty signatory. <b>Druman Patel</b> (M&amp;S Global) — Shanghai WFOE incorporation agent. <b>Doug Fischer</b> — Shanghai finance officer; the &ldquo;DF&rdquo; initials on the $352.47 screenshot. <b>Jess Mireau</b> (founding MD) — operational lead for Danone; <i>not implicated in the theft mechanisms; the office she ran was converted into a theft vehicle around her.</i></div></div>
    <div class="comp"><div class="cl">Conduit</div><div class="ct"><div class="castrow"><span class="mg d" aria-hidden="true">BM</span><div style="min-width:0"><div class="cn">Bernard D. Malaky</div><div class="cid">non-employee · Annex A.5</div></div></div></div>
      <div class="cb">Cardholder of record on the loop&rsquo;s conduit card per AMEX&rsquo;s own descriptor population, 242 entries 2015–2024. Not an employee; no signatory authority; no operational role. Physically present at Madwell&rsquo;s offices during the relevant period; correspondence with Eisenman exists in the record.</div></div>
  </div>

<!-- ============ PLATE 09 — PROOF DISCIPLINE ============ -->
<section class="plate wrap" id="s09">
  <header class="popen reveal"><div class="po-num">09</div><div class="po-main"><h2 class="po-title">Proof Discipline</h2><div class="po-coord">PLATE 09 · PROOF DISCIPLINE · §9–§11 · 110 FINDINGS</div><p class="po-lede">what reproduces from source, what is interpretation, and the five productions that close the gap</p></div></header>
  <p class="ph-kicker">The package&rsquo;s own rule, stated on its face: <i>&ldquo;If any single claim on the left does not reproduce when you check it, treat it as belonging on the right.&rdquo;</i> Every production target in §10 shares one shape — <b>a record the operator did not author</b>.</p>

  <div class="pi reveal">
    <div class="col prv">
      <h4>Proven — reproducible from source</h4>
      <ul>
        <li><b>$141.5M / 0.005%</b> three-witness unreported-revenue convergence; six confirmed filed years.</li>
        <li><b>$136.2M / 0.4%</b> eight-rail DataHole decomposition, two corroborating methods.</li>
        <li><b>A.6 closure identity</b> — $141.5M ≈ $136.2M + $4.75M, residual $594,632 / 0.42%.</li>
        <li><b>Books-side rails</b> — US wage doubling w/ 59999 contra ($4.68M, 2019); HK manufactured loss (−HK$9.15M, 2021).</li>
        <li><b>The HK rail is real money</b> — 747 payments, HK$157.1M, 99.9% ledger-reconciled, 90 verbatim reference matches.</li>
        <li><b>The 1711 architecture</b>, the Wilkins proxy regime, the Sojka exclusion — on the bank&rsquo;s and the company&rsquo;s own records.</li>
        <li><b>The shelter approval, the pre-modeled exit, the 2023 return&rsquo;s engineered lines</b> — in writing, with figures matching to the dollar.</li>
      </ul>
    </div>
    <div class="col inf">
      <h4>Inferred — not established by these documents</h4>
      <ul>
        <li><b>Where the Hong Kong money landed.</b><span class="redact" aria-hidden="true"></span> Every human-readable field was operator-authored; not one of 747 recipients independently confirmed. Resolves with BoA HK production.</li>
        <li><b>The Malaky-rail refund destination.</b><span class="redact" aria-hidden="true"></span> AMEX returns credit balances to a destination of the cardholder&rsquo;s choosing; the receiving statements are not in the record.</li>
        <li><b>Source 04: (a) or (d).</b><span class="redact" aria-hidden="true"></span> Eisenman beyond the §415 cap, or money exited Plan 99519 before rollover. Fidelity Record 77 isolates which.</li>
        <li><b>Intent, at any point.</b><span class="redact" aria-hidden="true"></span> Acts and instruments are documented; mental state is a proof question for trial.</li>
      </ul>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="frame reveal d1" style="margin-top:30px">

    <div class="dv-k" style="color:var(--acc-dk)">§12 · Closing</div>
    <div class="dv-t reveal" style="max-width:20ch">Seven years,<br>never compared.</div>
    <div class="dv-s reveal d1">&ldquo;The architecture operated for seven years because no one compared the recordkeeping systems against each other. Each system reconciled within itself. The disagreements existed only at the boundaries — and the boundaries had never been crossed. <b>This package is the crossing of those boundaries.</b>&rdquo;</div>

/* ============ chrome: progress + scrollspy + hotkeys ============ */
const pctEl = document.getElementById('pct'), progEl = document.getElementById('progress');
const sections = [...document.querySelectorAll('section.plate')];
const navLinks = [...document.querySelectorAll('.nav a')];
function onScroll(){
  const h = document.documentElement;
  const max = h.scrollHeight - innerHeight;
  const f = max > 0 ? Math.min(1, scrollY / max) : 0;
  progEl.style.width = (f*100) + '%';
  pctEl.textContent = String(Math.round(f*100)).padStart(3,'0');
  let cur = sections[0] && sections[0].id;
  for(const s of sections){ if(s.getBoundingClientRect().top < innerHeight*0.4) cur = s.id; }
  navLinks.forEach(a => a.classList.toggle('active', a.dataset.s === cur));
  const COORDS = {s00:'PLATE 00 · DOSSIER',toc:'CONTENTS · THREE ACTS',s01:'PLATE 01 · THE CLOSURE IDENTITY',s02:'PLATE 02 · UNREPORTED REVENUE',s03:'PLATE 03 · THE ARCHITECTURE',s04:'PLATE 04 · THE DATAHOLE',s05:'PLATE 05 · MECHANISMS',s06:'PLATE 06 · THE AMEX LOOP',s07:'PLATE 07 · CHRONOLOGY',s08:'PLATE 08 · ROLES & ACCESS',s09:'PLATE 09 · PROOF DISCIPLINE'};
  const ce = document.getElementById('coord'); if(ce && COORDS[cur]) ce.textContent = COORDS[cur];
  const se = document.getElementById('spn'); if(se) se.textContent = (cur && cur.startsWith('s')) ? cur.slice(1) : '··';
}
addEventListener('scroll', onScroll, {passive:true}); onScroll();
addEventListener('keydown', e => {
  if(e.target.matches('input,textarea') || e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey || e.altKey) return;
  if(/^[0-9]$/.test(e.key)){ const t = document.getElementById('s0'+e.key); if(t) t.scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'}); }
});

/* ============ reveal ============ */
const io = new IntersectionObserver(es => es.forEach(en => { if(en.isIntersecting){ en.target.classList.add('in'); io.unobserve(en.target);} }), {threshold:.12});
document.querySelectorAll('.reveal').forEach(el => io.observe(el));

/* ============ segmented toggles (generic slider) ============ */
function segInit(seg, cb){
  const btns = [...seg.querySelectorAll('button')], slider = seg.querySelector('.slider');
  function place(b){ slider.style.width = b.offsetWidth+'px'; slider.style.transform = 'translateX('+(b.offsetLeft-3)+'px)'; }
  btns.forEach(b => b.addEventListener('click', () => { btns.forEach(x=>x.classList.remove('on')); b.classList.add('on'); place(b); cb(b.dataset.era ?? b.dataset.v ?? ''); }));
  requestAnimationFrame(()=>place(seg.querySelector('button.on')||btns[0]));
}

const chartSegs = [...document.querySelectorAll('#railchart .seg2')];
if(railSeg) segInit(railSeg, era => {
  railRows.forEach(r => {
    if(r.classList.contains('tot')) return;
    let dim = false;
    if(era === 'old') dim = !r.hasAttribute('data-era-old');
    if(era === 'new') dim = !r.hasAttribute('data-era-new');
    r.style.opacity = dim ? .28 : 1;
  });
  chartSegs.forEach(sg => {
    let dim = false;
    if(era === 'old') dim = !sg.hasAttribute('data-era-old');
    if(era === 'new') dim = !sg.hasAttribute('data-era-new');
    sg.classList.toggle('dim2', dim);
  });
  railNote.innerHTML = NOTES[era] || NOTES.full;
});

/* ============ count-up numerals ============ */
const reduceMotion = matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;
function fmt(n){ return n.toLocaleString('en-US'); }
const cio = new IntersectionObserver(es => es.forEach(en => {
  if(!en.isIntersecting) return;
  const el = en.target; cio.unobserve(el);
  const t = parseInt(el.dataset.t, 10), p = el.dataset.p || '', dec = el.dataset.dec;
  const final = p + fmt(t) + (dec ? '.'+dec : '');
  if(reduceMotion){ el.textContent = final; return; }
  const dur = 1300, t0 = performance.now();
  (function tick(now){
    const f = Math.min(1, (now - t0) / dur), e = 1 - Math.pow(1 - f, 3);
    el.textContent = p + fmt(Math.round(t * e)) + (dec ? '.'+dec : '');
    if(f < 1) requestAnimationFrame(tick); else el.textContent = final;
  })(t0);
}), {threshold:.4});
document.querySelectorAll('.cnt').forEach(el => cio.observe(el));

/* stake-chip: days since the 10/3/2024 exit */
(function(){
  const el=document.getElementById('dexit'); if(!el) return;
  const d=Math.floor((Date.now()-new Date('2024-10-03T00:00:00'))/86400000);
  el.textContent='+'+d+'D';
})();

/* closure-identity calculator */
(function(){
  const i1=document.getElementById('c-in'), i2=document.getElementById('c-out'), i3=document.getElementById('c-debt');
  if(!i1) return;
  const o1=document.getElementById('o-in'), o2=document.getElementById('o-out'), o3=document.getElementById('o-debt');
  const res=document.getElementById('c-res'), pct=document.getElementById('c-pct'), st=document.getElementById('c-state');
  const D=[141547013,136202381,4750000];
  function f(n){return '$'+Math.round(n).toLocaleString('en-US')}
  function upd(){
    const a=+i1.value,b=+i2.value,c=+i3.value;
    o1.value=f(a); o2.value=f(b); o3.value=f(c);
    const r=a-(b+c), p=Math.abs(r)/a*100;
    res.textContent=f(r); pct.textContent=p.toFixed(3)+'%';
    st.className='cstate'+(p<=0.5?' ok':(p<=2?'':' fail'));
    st.textContent=p<=0.5?'CONVERGENCE-GRADE · < 0.5%':(p<=2?'APPROXIMATE · 0.5–2%':'HYPOTHESIS FAILS · > 2%');
  }
  [i1,i2,i3].forEach(i=>i.addEventListener('input',upd));
  document.getElementById('c-reset').addEventListener('click',()=>{[i1,i2,i3].forEach((i,k)=>i.value=D[k]);upd();});
  upd();
})();
/* verify.py cached console */
(function(){
  const q=document.getElementById('t-q'), run=document.getElementById('t-run'), out=document.getElementById('t-out');
  if(!q) return;
  async function show(id){
    id=id.trim();
    // live helper first (verify_server.py on 127.0.0.1:5077), cached fallback
    try{
      const ctl=new AbortController(); setTimeout(()=>ctl.abort(),900);
      const r=await fetch('http://127.0.0.1:5077/verify',{method:'POST',headers:{'Content-Type':'application/json'},body:JSON.stringify({id}),signal:ctl.signal});
      if(r.ok){ const j=await r.json(); out.textContent='$ python3 verify.py --only '+id+'   (LIVE)\n\n'+j.out; return; }
    }catch(e){/* helper absent — cached mode */}
    const c=VCACHE[id];
    let txt='$ python3 verify.py --only '+id+'\n\n';
    if(c){ txt+=c.block+'\n\n  TOTAL: 1 findings | 1 '+c.status+' | 0 FAIL | 0 SKIP | 0 ERROR'; }
    else{
      const row=FINDINGS.find(x=>x.id.toLowerCase()===id.toLowerCase());
      txt += row ? '  ['+row.st+']  '+row.id+'  '+row.l+'\n\n  (register row — full detail block not in the page cache; run locally for the complete output)'
                 : '  unknown finding id: '+id+'\n  valid ids: P1, A.1–A.6, B.1–B.84, C.1.*  (110 findings)';
    }
    out.textContent=txt;
  }
  run.addEventListener('click',()=>show(q.value));
  q.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{if(e.key==='Enter')show(q.value)});
  document.querySelectorAll('.fchip[data-t]').forEach(b=>b.addEventListener('click',()=>{q.value=b.dataset.t;show(b.dataset.t)}));
  show('A.6');
})();


/* ============ data-texture fields ============ */
/* 943 reconciliation PDFs — the perimeter, drawn */
(function(){
  const host = document.getElementById('field943'); if(!host) return;
  const cols = 41, rows = 23, sz = 7, g = 4, GW = cols*(sz+g)-g, GH = rows*(sz+g)-g;
  const PX = 26, PY = 34;                       // perimeter padding
  const W = GW + PX*2 + 250, H = GH + PY*2 + 26;
  let cells = '';
  for(let i=0;i<943;i++){
    const x = PX+(i%cols)*(sz+g), y = PY+Math.floor(i/cols)*(sz+g);
    cells += `<rect x="${x}" y="${y}" width="${sz}" height="${sz}" rx="1" fill="var(--ink-4)"/>`;
  }
  const ox = GW + PX*2 + 92, oy = PY + GH/2 - 8;  // the account outside the wall
  host.innerHTML = `<svg width="${W}" height="${H}" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}" role="img"
    aria-label="943 reconciliation PDFs inside a drawn perimeter; account 1072 outside it" style="max-width:100%;height:auto">
    <rect x="8" y="10" width="${GW+PX*2-16}" height="${GH+PY*2-16}" fill="none" stroke="var(--ink)" stroke-width="1.5"/>
    <text x="${PX}" y="${PY-12}" style="font-family:var(--mono);font-size:9.5px;letter-spacing:.14em;fill:var(--ink)">RECONCILIATION PERIMETER — M&amp;S GLOBAL, FIRM OF RECORD</text>
    <text x="${GW+PX-2}" y="${PY+GH+22}" text-anchor="end" style="font-family:var(--mono);font-size:9px;fill:var(--ink-3)">41 × 23 = 943 PDFS · 2018–2024</text>
    ${cells}
    <line x1="${GW+PX*2-8}" y1="${oy+8}" x2="${ox-14}" y2="${oy+8}" stroke="var(--ink-3)" stroke-width="1" stroke-dasharray="2 4"/>
    <rect x="${ox}" y="${oy}" width="${sz+9}" height="${sz+9}" rx="1.5" fill="var(--flag)" stroke="var(--ink)" stroke-width="1.4"/>
    <text x="${ox-7}" y="${oy+44}" style="font-family:var(--mono);font-size:9.5px;fill:var(--ink);letter-spacing:.1em;font-weight:600">ACCOUNT 1072</text>
    <text x="${ox-7}" y="${oy+58}" style="font-family:var(--mono);font-size:8.5px;fill:var(--ink-2);letter-spacing:.06em">NEVER INSIDE</text>
  </svg>`;
})();
/* 747 payments — a register with a zero readout */
(function(){
  const host = document.getElementById('field747'); if(!host) return;
  const perRow = 83, rows = 9, dx = 10, dy = 24, GW = perRow*dx, GH = rows*dy;
  const W = GW + 240, H = GH + 14;
  let t = '';
  for(let r=0;r<rows;r++){
    const n0 = r*perRow+1, n1 = Math.min((r+1)*perRow, 747);
    t += `<line x1="0" y1="${r*dy+19}" x2="${GW}" y2="${r*dy+19}" stroke="var(--line)" stroke-width="1"/>`;
    t += `<text x="${GW+12}" y="${r*dy+16}" style="font-family:var(--mono);font-size="8px";font-size:8px;fill:var(--ink-4)">${String(n0).padStart(3,'0')}–${String(n1).padStart(3,'0')}</text>`;
  }
  for(let i=0;i<747;i++){
    const x=(i%perRow)*dx+2, y=Math.floor(i/perRow)*dy+4;
    t += `<line x1="${x}" y1="${y}" x2="${x}" y2="${y+12}" stroke="var(--ink-3)" stroke-width="1.5"/>`;
  }
  host.innerHTML = `<svg width="${W}" height="${H}" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}" role="img" aria-label="747 settled payments; confirmed recipients zero" style="max-width:100%;height:auto">
    ${t}
    <g>
      <rect x="${GW+88}" y="${GH/2-34}" width="138" height="64" fill="var(--paper)" stroke="var(--ink)" stroke-width="1.5"/>
      <text x="${GW+157}" y="${GH/2-12}" text-anchor="middle" style="font-family:var(--mono);font-size:9px;letter-spacing:.16em;fill:var(--ink-3)">CONFIRMED</text>
      <text x="${GW+157}" y="${GH/2+14}" text-anchor="middle" style="font-family:var(--mono);font-size:20px;font-weight:600;fill:var(--ink)">000<tspan fill="var(--ink-4)">/747</tspan></text>
    </g>
  </svg>`;
})();

/* ============ circuit hover isolation ============ */
const circuit = document.getElementById('circuit');
if(circuit){
  const groups = [...circuit.querySelectorAll('.grp')];
  const cards = [...document.querySelectorAll('.mech .comp[data-step]')];
  function hot(step){
    circuit.classList.toggle('dimmed', !!step);
    groups.forEach(g => g.classList.toggle('hot', g.dataset.step === step));
    cards.forEach(c => c.style.borderColor = c.dataset.step === step ? 'var(--accent)' : '');
  }
  groups.forEach(g => { g.addEventListener('mouseenter', ()=>hot(g.dataset.step)); g.addEventListener('mouseleave', ()=>hot(null)); });
  cards.forEach(c => { c.addEventListener('mouseenter', ()=>hot(c.dataset.step)); c.addEventListener('mouseleave', ()=>hot(null)); });
}

/* ============ findings browser ============ */
const fbl = document.getElementById('fblist'), fbn = document.getElementById('fbn'), fbq = document.getElementById('fbq');
let fSt = '', fCl = '', fQ = '';
function fclass(id){ return id === 'P1' ? 'P' : id[0]; }
function renderF(){
  if(!fbl) return;
  const q = fQ.trim().toLowerCase();
  const rows = FINDINGS.filter(f =>
    (!fSt || f.st === fSt) &&
    (!fCl || fclass(f.id) === fCl) &&
    (!q || (f.id + ' ' + f.l).toLowerCase().includes(q))
  );
  fbn.textContent = rows.length;
  fbl.innerHTML = rows.map(f =>
    '<div class="fb-row"><span class="fid">'+f.id+'</span><span class="fst '+f.st+'">'+(f.st==='TIMEOUT'?'T/O°':f.st)+'</span><span class="fl2">'+f.l.replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;')+'</span></div>'
  ).join('') || '<div class="fb-row"><span></span><span></span><span class="fl2" style="color:var(--ink-3)">no matches</span></div>';
}
if(fbq){
  fbq.addEventListener('input', () => { fQ = fbq.value; renderF(); });
  segInit(document.getElementById('fbst'), v => { fSt = v; renderF(); });
  segInit(document.getElementById('fbcl'), v => { fCl = v; renderF(); });
  renderF();
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
